-invariant, it induces a Riemannian structure
\left<\cdot,\cdot\right>_{M}
on
M
, making it a homogeneous space with non-positive sectional curvature. We refer to
[
24
, Appendix A, Appendix C]
for more details. We call
\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}
the Levi–Civita connection on
M
.

Let
\tilde{g}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow G
defined at Lemma
5.8
. Recall that it satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\tilde{g}(0)&=&\mathrm{Id}_{E},\\
\tilde{g}(t)^{-1}\tilde{g}^{\prime}(t)&=&\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)).\end{array}\right.

Moreover, for all
t
,
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)=\tilde{g}(t)^{-1}\cdot b
.

For all
g\in G
, there is a unique
\xi\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}
such that
\pi(g)=\pi(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{\xi})
. In this case,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{\xi}
is given by the Hermitian definite positive part of
g
(on the left) in the polar decomposition of
g
. For all
t
, we write,

\gamma(t)=\pi(\tilde{g}(t))\in M

and, when
0\leqslant s\leqslant t
,

\tilde{g}(s)^{-1}\tilde{g}(t)=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{-\xi(s,t)}u(s,t),\qquad\textrm{so }\pi(\tilde{g}(s)^{-1}\tilde{g}(t))=\pi(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{-\xi(s,t)}).

(19)

When
0\leqslant s\leqslant t
, we also denote by
\gamma_{s,t}:[s,t]\rightarrow M
the unique geodesic such that
\gamma_{s,t}(s)=\gamma(s)
and
\gamma_{s,t}(t)=\gamma(t)
. For all
s\leqslant r\leqslant t
, we have

\gamma_{s,t}(r)=\pi\left(\tilde{g}(s)\exp\left(-\frac{r-s}{t-s}\xi(s,t)\right)\right).

(20)

Let for all
0\leqslant s\leqslant r\leqslant t
,
\rho_{s,t}(r)=d_{M}(\gamma_{s,t}(r),\gamma(r))
where
d_{M}
is the Riemannian distance on
M
.

Step 2 :
There are constants
C>0
and
0<\epsilon<1
such that for all
t>0
,

\int_{t}^{+\infty}\left\|\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right\|_{M}\,ds\leqslant\frac{C}{t^{\epsilon}}.

Indeed, we have for all
t
,
\tilde{g}(t)^{-1}\tilde{g}^{\prime}(t)=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))
. By
[
24
, Theorem C.1]
, it implies that

\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}\gamma^{\prime}(t)=d\pi(\tilde{g}(t))\tilde{g}(t)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}d\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\frac{\partial\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}}{\partial t}(b,t).

Let
C_{1}>0
be a constant that bounds
\left\|d\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\right\|
for all
t
, where
\left\|\cdot\right\|
is the operator norm from
(T_{\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)}B,L^{2})
to
(\mathfrak{k},\left<\cdot,\cdot\right>)
.
C_{1}
exists because the flow converges thus stays in a compact set. We have

\left\|\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}\gamma^{\prime}(t)\right\|_{M}=\left\|\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}d\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\frac{\partial\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}}{\partial t}(b,t)\right\|\leqslant C_{1}\left\|\frac{\partial\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}}{\partial t}(b,t)\right\|_{L^{2}}.

The assumption of Step 2 follows from the inequality in Proposition
5.11
. We may always assume
\epsilon<1
up to increasing the constant
C
.

Step 3 :
For all
0<s<r<t
, if
\rho_{s,t}(r)\neq 0
, then
-\frac{C}{s^{\epsilon}}\leqslant\rho_{s,t}^{\prime}(r)\leqslant\frac{C}{r^{\epsilon}}
.

Since
\rho_{s,t}(s)=0
, there is a
s\leqslant r_{1}<r
such that
\rho_{s,t}(r_{1})=0
and
\rho_{s,t}
does not vanish on
]r_{1},r]
. By
[
24
, Lemma A.3]
,
\rho_{s,t}
is continuous and smooth at every point where it does not vanish, differentiable on the left and on the right at every point where it vanishes, and for all
u\in]r_{1},r]
,

\rho_{s,t}^{\prime\prime}(u)\geqslant-\left\|\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}\gamma^{\prime}(u)\right\|_{M},\qquad\frac{d\rho_{s,t}}{dr^{+}}(r_{1})\geqslant 0.

Here,
\frac{d\rho_{s,t}}{dr^{+}}(r_{1})
denotes the derivative on the right at
r_{1}
of
\rho_{s,t}
. Therefore, by integrating and by Step 2,

\rho_{s,t}^{\prime}(r)=\frac{d\rho_{s,t}}{dr^{+}}(r_{1})+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\rho_{s,t}^{\prime\prime}(u)\,du\geqslant-\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\left\|\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}\gamma^{\prime}(u)\right\|_{M}\,du\geqslant-\frac{C}{r_{1}^{\epsilon}}\geqslant-\frac{C}{s^{\epsilon}}.

Similarly, we can find a
r<r_{2}\leqslant t
such that
\rho_{s,t}(r_{2})=0
and
\rho_{s,t}
does not vanish on
[r,r_{2}[
. By
[
24
, Lemma A.3]
,
\frac{d\rho_{s,t}}{dr^{-}}(r_{2})\leqslant 0
, so with the same arguments,

\rho_{s,t}^{\prime}(r)=\frac{d\rho_{s,t}}{dr^{-}}(r_{2})-\int_{r}^{r_{2}}\rho_{s,t}^{\prime\prime}(u)\,du\leqslant\int_{r}^{r_{2}}\left\|\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}\gamma^{\prime}(u)\right\|_{M}\,du\leqslant\frac{C}{r^{\epsilon}}.

Step 4 :
There is a constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that for all
0\leqslant s\leqslant r\leqslant t
,
\rho_{s,t}(r)\leqslant C^{\prime}(r^{1-\epsilon}-s^{1-\epsilon})
.

It is clearly true for any
C^{\prime}
if
\rho_{s,t}(r)=0
. Else, set
s\leqslant r_{0}<r
such that
\rho_{s,t}(r_{0})=0
and
\rho_{s,t}
does not vanish on
]r_{0},r]
. Once more, it exists because
\rho_{s,t}(s)=0
. By integrating the inequality of Step 3 over
]r_{0},r]
, we obtain

\rho_{s,t}(r)=\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\rho_{s,t}^{\prime}(u)\,du\leqslant\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{C}{u^{\epsilon}}\,du=\frac{C}{1-\epsilon}(r^{1-\epsilon}-r_{0}^{1-\epsilon})\leqslant C^{\prime}(r^{1-\epsilon}-s^{1-\epsilon})

with
C^{\prime}=\frac{C}{1-\epsilon}
.

Step 5 :
For all
0\leqslant s\leqslant t
, we set
\eta(s,t)=u(s,t)^{\dagger}\xi(s,t)u(s,t)\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}
. We have

\gamma_{s,t}^{\prime}(s)=d\pi(\tilde{g}(s))\tilde{g}(s)\left(-\frac{\xi(s,t)}{t-s}\right),\qquad\gamma_{s,t}^{\prime}(t)=d\pi(\tilde{g}(t))\tilde{g}(t)\left(-\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}\right).

The first equality comes from (
19
). Using (
20
) and (
19
), we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{s,t}(r)

\displaystyle=\pi\left(\tilde{g}(s)\exp\left(-\frac{r-s}{t-s}\xi(s,t)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\pi\left(\tilde{g}(t)u(s,t)^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{\xi(s,t)}\exp\left(-\frac{r-s}{t-s}\xi(s,t)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\pi\left(\tilde{g}(t)\exp\left(-\frac{r-t}{t-s}u(s,t)^{\dagger}\xi(s,t)u(s,t)\right)u(s,t)^{\dagger}\right)

\displaystyle=\pi\left(\tilde{g}(t)\exp\left(-\frac{r-t}{t-s}\eta(s,t)\right)\right).

We deduce the second equality.

Step 6 :
For all
s^{\prime}\geqslant s\geqslant 0
and all
t\geqslant s^{\prime}+1
,

\left\|\frac{\eta(s^{\prime},t)}{t-s^{\prime}}-\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}\right\|\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}({s^{\prime}}^{1-\epsilon}-s^{1-\epsilon})}{t-s^{\prime}}.

Indeed,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\eta(s^{\prime},t)}{t-s^{\prime}}-\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|d\pi(\tilde{g}(t))\tilde{g}(t)\frac{\eta(s^{\prime},t)}{t-s^{\prime}}-d\pi(\tilde{g}(t))\tilde{g}(t)\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}\right\|_{M}

\displaystyle=\left\|-\gamma_{s^{\prime},t}^{\prime}(t)+\gamma_{s,t}^{\prime}(t)\right\|_{M}\textrm{ by Step 5,}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{d_{M}(\gamma_{s^{\prime},t}(s^{\prime}),\gamma_{s,t}(s^{\prime}))}{t-s^{\prime}}\textrm{ by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GRS}{}{}, Lemma A.4]},}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho_{s,t}(s^{\prime})}{t-s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}({s^{\prime}}^{1-\epsilon}-s^{1-\epsilon})}{t-s^{\prime}}\textrm{ by Step 4.}

Step 7 :
For all
0\leqslant s\leqslant t
,

\left\|\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\right\|\leqslant\frac{C}{s^{\epsilon}}.

Indeed,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|d\pi(\tilde{g}(t))\tilde{g}(t)\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}+d\pi(\tilde{g}(t))\tilde{g}(t)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\right\|_{M}

\displaystyle=\left\|-\gamma_{s,t}^{\prime}(t)+\gamma^{\prime}(t)\right\|_{M}\textrm{ by Step 5.}

Then, the inequality clearly holds if
\gamma_{s,t}^{\prime}(t)=\gamma^{\prime}(t)
. Else, by
[
24
, Lemma A.3]
, we have

\frac{d}{dr^{-}}\rho_{s,t}(t)=-\left\|-\gamma_{s,t}^{\prime}(t)+\gamma^{\prime}(t)\right\|_{M}<0.

We deduce that in a left punctured neighbourhood of
t
,
\rho_{s,t}>0
and

\left\|-\gamma_{s,t}^{\prime}(t)+\gamma^{\prime}(t)\right\|_{M}=-\frac{d\rho_{s,t}}{dr^{-}}(t)=-\lim_{r\rightarrow t^{-}}\rho_{s,t}^{\prime}(r).

By Step 3, for all
r<t
sufficiently close to
t
,
\rho_{s,t}^{\prime}(r)\geqslant-\frac{C}{s^{\epsilon}}
because
\rho_{s,t}(r)\neq 0
. We deduce the wanted inequality.

Step 8 :
For all fixed
s\geqslant 0
,
\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}\underset{t\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})
.

We take
t>\max\{1,s^{2}\}
and we apply Step 7 with
\sqrt{t}\rightarrow s
and Step 6 with
\sqrt{t}\rightarrow s^{\prime}
.

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}-\frac{\eta(\sqrt{t},t)}{t-\sqrt{t}}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\eta(\sqrt{t},t)}{t-\sqrt{t}}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\right\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}(\sqrt{t}^{1-\epsilon}-s^{1-\epsilon})}{t-\sqrt{t}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}^{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\underset{t\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

Since
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\underset{t\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}\tilde{\mu}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})
, we deduce the wanted result.

Step 9 :
For all
s\geqslant 0
and all
t^{\prime}\geqslant t\geqslant s+1
,

\left\|\frac{\xi(s,t^{\prime})}{t^{\prime}-s}-\frac{\xi(s,t)}{t-s}\right\|\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}}{t^{\epsilon}}.

In particular, by completeness of
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}
,
\xi_{\infty}(s)=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{\xi(s,t)}{t-s}
exists.

Indeed,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\xi(s,t^{\prime})}{t^{\prime}-s}-\frac{\xi(s,t)}{t-s}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|d\pi(\tilde{g}(s))\pi(\tilde{g}(s))\frac{\xi(s,t^{\prime})}{t^{\prime}-s}-d\pi(\tilde{g}(s))\pi(\tilde{g}(s))\frac{\xi(s,t)}{t-s}\right\|_{M}

\displaystyle=\left\|-\gamma_{s,t^{\prime}}^{\prime}(s)+\gamma_{s,t}^{\prime}(s)\right\|_{M}\textrm{ by Step 5,}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{d_{M}(\gamma_{s,t}(t),\gamma_{s,t^{\prime}}(t))}{t-s}\textrm{ by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GRS}{}{}, Lemma A.4]},}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho_{s,t^{\prime}}(t)}{t-s}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}(t^{1-\epsilon}-s^{1-\epsilon})}{t-s}\textrm{ by Step 4,}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}}{t^{\epsilon}}.

Step 10 :

\xi_{\infty}(s)\underset{s\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})
.

By Step 9 applied at
t=s+1
and
t^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty
, we have, for all
s\geqslant 0

\left\|\xi_{\infty}(s)-\xi(s,s+1)\right\|\leqslant\frac{C^{\prime}}{(s+1)^{\epsilon}}.

Then,

\displaystyle\left\|\xi(s,s+1)+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,s))\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|d\pi(\tilde{g}(s))\tilde{g}(s)\xi(s,s+1)+d\pi(\tilde{g}(s))\tilde{g}(s)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,s))\right\|_{M}

\displaystyle=\left\|-\gamma_{s,s+1}^{\prime}(s)+\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right\|_{M}\textrm{ by Step 5.}

Then, using the same reasoning as in Step 7,
\left\|-\gamma_{s,s+1}^{\prime}(s)+\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right\|_{M}=0
or

\displaystyle\left\|-\gamma_{s,s+1}^{\prime}(s)+\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right\|_{M}

\displaystyle=\lim_{r\rightarrow s^{+}}\rho_{s,s+1}^{\prime}(r)\textrm{ by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GRS}{}{}, Lemma A.3]},}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{s^{\epsilon}}\textrm{ by Step 3.}

We deduce that
\xi_{\infty}(s)\underset{s\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})
.

Step 11 :
Conclusion.

Recall that for all
t>s\geqslant 0
,

\frac{\eta(s,t)}{t-s}=u(s,t)^{\dagger}\frac{\xi(s,t)}{t-s}u(s,t).

Let
t_{s,p}\underset{p\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}+\infty
such that
u_{\infty}(s)=\lim_{p\rightarrow+\infty}u(s,t_{s,p})\in K
exists. We then have, by Steps 8 and 9,
-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})=u_{\infty}(s)^{\dagger}\xi_{\infty}(s)u_{\infty}(s)
. Let
\tilde{s}_{k}\rightarrow+\infty
be a sequence such that
u_{\infty,\infty}=\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}u_{\infty}(\tilde{s}_{k})\in K
exists. By Step 10, we have

-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})=-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}u_{\infty,\infty}^{\dagger}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})u_{\infty,\infty}.

Let for all integer
k
,
\tilde{b}_{k}=u_{\infty,\infty}u_{\infty}(\tilde{s}_{k})^{\dagger}\cdot\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\tilde{s}_{k})\in K\cdot\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\tilde{s}_{k})
. We clearly have
\tilde{b}_{k}\rightarrow\tilde{b}_{\infty}
. Let us fix
k
and set for all
p
,

\tilde{b}_{k,p}=u_{\infty,\infty}u(\tilde{s}_{k},t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p})^{\dagger}\cdot\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\tilde{s}_{k})\underset{p\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}\tilde{b}_{k}.

We have

\frac{1}{t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p}}u_{\infty,\infty}\eta(\tilde{s}_{k},t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p})u_{\infty,\infty}^{\dagger}\underset{p\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}u_{\infty,\infty}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})u_{\infty,\infty}^{\dagger}=-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})

and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{u_{\infty,\infty}\eta(\tilde{s}_{k},t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p})u_{\infty,\infty}^{\dagger}}\cdot\tilde{b}_{k,p}

\displaystyle=u_{\infty,\infty}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{\eta(\tilde{s}_{k},t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p})}u_{\infty,\infty}^{\dagger}u_{\infty,\infty}u(\tilde{s}_{k},t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p})^{\dagger}\cdot\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\tilde{s}_{k})

\displaystyle=u_{\infty,\infty}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{\eta(\tilde{s}_{k},t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p})}u(\tilde{s}_{k},t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p})^{\dagger}\cdot\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\tilde{s}_{k})

\displaystyle=u_{\infty,\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p})

\displaystyle\underset{p\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}u_{\infty,\infty}\tilde{b}_{\infty}\in B_{2}.

Therefore, we may apply Proposition
5.3
with the sequences,

\tilde{b}_{k,p}\underset{p\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}\tilde{b}_{k},\qquad\frac{1}{t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p}}u_{\infty,\infty}\eta(\tilde{s}_{k},t_{\tilde{s}_{k},p})u_{\infty,\infty}^{\dagger}\underset{p\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty}),

to conclude that
t\mapsto\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}t\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})}\cdot\tilde{b}_{k}
remains bounded and converges in
B_{2}
.
∎

6
Proof of the main theorem

Let
B_{2,0}
,
\mathcal{U}_{d}
and
\mathcal{B}_{d}
as determined in Section
5
. Let
B_{2}\subset B_{2,0}
and
B_{1}\subset B_{2}
also determined in Section
5
. We prove here Theorem
4.12
for all
(\zeta,b)\in\mathcal{U}_{d}\times B_{1}
such that the flows
\phi_{\zeta}
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}
are defined at all time and stay in a compact subset of
B_{2}
.

6.1
Equivalence of semi-stabilities

We prove here the equivalence of Theorem
4.12
about the different notions of semi-stabilities, still under the assumptions that
\phi_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
exist at all time and converge. We also show that in these cases, the limit points of these flows are zeroes of the associated moment maps.

1\Rightarrow 3
Assume that
\mu_{\zeta}
vanishes in
\overline{G\cdot b}\cap B_{2}
. Let
b^{\prime}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{2}
be any point of the orbit of
b
. Let
F\subset E
be an admissible sub-bundle of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
. Since
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{E}_{b}
, with an isomorphism in
G
, there is an admissible sub-bundle
F^{\prime}\subset E
of
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
such that
(F^{\prime},\overline{\partial}_{b^{\prime}})
is isomorphic to
(F,\overline{\partial}_{b})
. Let
\xi=-\mathrm{rk}({F^{\prime}}^{\bot})\Pi_{F^{\prime}}+\mathrm{rk}(F^{\prime})\Pi_{{F^{\prime}}^{\bot}}
where
\Pi_{F^{\prime}}
is the orthogonal projection onto
F^{\prime}
and
\Pi_{{F^{\prime}}^{\bot}}=\mathrm{Id}_{E}-\Pi_{F^{\prime}}
is the orthogonal projection onto
{F^{\prime}}^{\bot}
.
F^{\prime}
is admissible thus
\xi\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}
. Let for all
t
,

w(t)=\left<\mu_{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>.

By Proposition
5.3
, for all
t
,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}\in B_{3,0}
and it converges toward some
b_{\infty}\in B_{3,0}
. Moreover, still by Proposition
5.3
,
(F,\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}})\subset\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
and
(F^{\bot},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}})\subset\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
are holomorphic thus, in the orthogonal decomposition
E=F\oplus F^{\bot}
,

\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(E,\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h)=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(F^{\prime},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h)&0\\
0&\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}({F^{\prime}}^{\bot},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h)\end{pmatrix}.

Recall that
\mu_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=\Pi_{\mathfrak{k}}\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(b_{\infty})
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\in\mathfrak{k}
so
\left<\mu_{\zeta}(b_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>=\left<\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(b_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>
. We deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}w(t)

\displaystyle=\left<\mu_{\zeta}(b_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>

\displaystyle=-2\pi\int_{X}\mathrm{tr}(-\mathrm{rk}({F^{\prime}}^{\bot})\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(F^{\prime},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h)+\mathrm{rk}({F^{\prime}})\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}({F^{\prime}}^{\bot},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h))

\displaystyle=2\pi\mathrm{rk}({F^{\prime}}^{\bot})P_{\zeta}(F^{\prime})-2\pi\mathrm{rk}(F^{\prime})P_{\zeta}({F^{\prime}}^{\bot})

\displaystyle=2\pi(\mathrm{rk}(E)-\mathrm{rk}(F))P_{\zeta}(F)-2\pi\mathrm{rk}(F)(P_{\zeta}(E)-P_{\zeta}(F))

\displaystyle=2\pi\mathrm{rk}(E)P_{\zeta}(F).

Moreover, for all
t
,

w^{\prime}(t)=\left<d\mu_{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime})L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}\xi,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>=\Omega_{\zeta,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}\xi,L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}\xi)\leqslant 0.

We deduce that
2\pi\mathrm{rk}(E)P_{\zeta}(F)=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}w(t)\leqslant w(0)
and

w(0)=\left<\mu_{\zeta}(b^{\prime}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>\leqslant\left\|\mu_{\zeta}(b^{\prime})\right\|\left\|\xi\right\|=\left\|\mu_{\zeta}(b^{\prime})\right\|\sqrt{\mathrm{rk}(E)\mathrm{rk}(F)\mathrm{rk}(F^{\bot})\mathrm{Vol}(X)},

hence
\left\|\mu_{\zeta}(b^{\prime})\right\|\geqslant 2\pi\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{rk}(E)}{\mathrm{rk}(F)\mathrm{rk}(F^{\bot})\mathrm{Vol}(X)}}P_{\zeta}(F)
. It is true for all
b^{\prime}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{2}
. By assumption,
\left\|\mu_{\zeta}\right\|
takes arbitrarily low positive values on this orbit so
P_{\zeta}(F)\leqslant 0
, proving the local
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stability.

2\Rightarrow 3
Assume that there is a
\overline{\partial}\in\overline{\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b}}\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}
such that
\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(\overline{\partial})=0
. Let
\overline{\partial}_{m}\rightarrow\overline{\partial}
be a sequence of elements of
\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b}\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}
which converge toward
\overline{\partial}
. Let
\beta:\mathcal{B}_{d}\rightarrow B_{3,0}
be the smooth function given by Proposition
5.6
. Recall that
\beta(\overline{\partial}_{m})\in G\cdot b\cap B_{3,0}
and by continuity of
\beta
,
b_{\infty}=\beta(\overline{\partial})\in\overline{G\cdot b}\cap B_{3,0}
. Moreover, as
\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(\overline{\partial})=0
, we have
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=0
.

It shows that
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}
vanishes on
\overline{G\cdot b}\cap B_{3,0}
. We then apply the same method as for
1\Rightarrow 3
but we replace
\mu_{\zeta}
by
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}
.

3\Rightarrow 1
and
2
The proof is the same for
1
and
2
so we only prove it for
2
. Assume that
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stable. Let
b_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)\in B_{2}
. Let us show that
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=0
.

Assume it is not the case. By Proposition
5.14
, there is a
b^{\prime}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{2}
such that at all non-negative time,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}t\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})}\cdot b^{\prime}\in B_{2}
. Let
\lambda_{1}<\cdots<\lambda_{m}
be the increasing eigenvalues of
-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})
and

G_{k}=\ker(\lambda_{k}\mathrm{Id}_{E}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})),\qquad F_{k}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}G_{i},

the associated eigenspaces. Since
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})\neq 0
and
\mathrm{tr}(\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty}))=0
, we must have
m\geqslant 2
. By Proposition
5.3
, the
F_{k}
form a filtration of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
by admissible sub-bundles. Moreover, each
(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}})
is a holomorphic sub-bundle of
\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
because
L_{b_{\infty}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=0
. It is a consequence of Proposition
5.11
which says that
b_{\infty}
must be a critical point of
\tilde{f}_{\zeta}
. In particular, we have the orthogonal and holomorphic decomposition

\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{m}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}}).

(21)

Let for all
k
,
\Pi_{k}
be the orthogonal projection onto
G_{k}
. We have

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left<\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\lambda_{k}\Pi_{k}\right>

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}-2\pi\lambda_{k}\int_{X}\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h))\textrm{ by (\ref{EQ:Décomposition E_binfty}),}

\displaystyle=-2\pi\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda_{k}P_{\zeta}(G_{k})

\displaystyle=-2\pi\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k+1})P_{\zeta}(F_{k})\textrm{ because }P_{\zeta}(E)=0,

\displaystyle\leqslant 0\textrm{ by local $P_{\zeta}$-semi-stability of $\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}$.}

Since
\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})\right\|^{2}>0
, we reach a contradiction. We deduce that
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=0
. In particular,
\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,b_{\infty})\in\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}}\subset\overline{\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b}}
is a solution to the
P_{\zeta}
-critical equation and by Proposition
5.6
, it belongs to
\mathcal{B}_{d}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stable in
\mathcal{B}_{d}
.

6.2
Equivalence of polystabilities

We prove here the equivalence of Theorem
4.12
about the different notions of polystabilities, still under the assumptions that the flows
\phi_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
exist in
B_{2}
at all non-negative time and converge in
B_{2}
.

1\Rightarrow 3
Assume that
\mu_{\zeta}
vanishes in
G\cdot b\cap B_{2}
. Let
b^{\prime}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{2}\subset B_{3,0}
be a zero of
\mu_{\zeta}
. Since
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
(with the isomorphism in
G
),
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-polystable if and only if
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
is.

Let
F\subset E
be an admissible sub-bundle of
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
and
\xi=-\mathrm{rk}(F^{\bot})\Pi_{F}+\mathrm{rk}(F)\Pi_{F^{\bot}}
where
\Pi_{F}
is orthogonal projection onto
F
and
\Pi_{F^{\bot}}=\mathrm{Id}_{E}-\Pi_{F}
is the orthogonal projection onto
F^{\bot}
.
F
is admissible thus
\xi\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}
. Let for all
t
,

w(t)=\left<\mu_{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>.

We have
w(0)=0
and by Proposition
5.3
, for all
t
,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}\in B_{3,0}
and it converges toward some
b_{\infty}\in B_{3,0}
. Moreover,
(F,\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}})\subset\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
and
(F^{\bot},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}})\subset\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
are holomorphic thus, in the orthogonal decomposition
E=F\oplus F^{\bot}
,

\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(E,\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h)=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(F,\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h)&0\\
0&\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(F^{\bot},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h)\end{pmatrix}.

Recall that
\mu_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=\Pi_{\mathfrak{k}}\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(b_{\infty})
and
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\in\mathfrak{k}
so
\left<\mu_{\zeta}(b_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>=\left<\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(b_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>
. We deduce that

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}w(t)=\left<\mu_{\zeta}(b_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>=2\pi\mathrm{rk}(E)P_{\zeta}(F).

Moreover, for all
t
,

w^{\prime}(t)=\left<d\mu_{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime})L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}\xi,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>=\Omega_{\zeta,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}\xi,L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}\xi)\leqslant 0

with equality of and only if
L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}\xi=0
. We deduce that
\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}w(t)\leqslant w(0)=0
with equality if and only if for all
t
,
L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}}\xi=0
. It implies that
P_{\zeta}(F)\leqslant 0
and in case of equality,
\xi
is
\overline{\partial}_{b^{\prime}}
-holomorphic so
F^{\bot}=\ker(-\mathrm{rk}(F^{\bot})\mathrm{Id}_{E}-\xi)
is an admissible sub-bundle of
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
hence the orthogonal and holomorphic decomposition
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}=(F,\overline{\partial}_{b^{\prime}})\oplus(F^{\bot},\overline{\partial}_{b^{\prime}})
. It shows that
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
, thus
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-polystable.

2\Rightarrow 3
Assume that
\mu_{\infty,\zeta}
vanishes on
\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)\cdot b\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}
and let
\overline{\partial}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)\cdot b\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}
such that
\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(\overline{\partial})=0
. By Proposition
5.6
, there is a
b^{\prime}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{3,0}
such that
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b^{\prime})=0
. We then apply the same method as for
1\Rightarrow 3
but with

w(t)=\left<\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>.

3\Rightarrow 1
and
2
The proof is the same for
1
and
2
so we only prove it for
2
. Assume that
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-polystable. Let
b_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
. We proved in Sub-section
6.1
that
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=0
.

In this case, it is a usual result that
\mathrm{Stab}(b_{\infty})=(\mathrm{Stab}(b_{\infty})\cap K)^{\mathbb{C}}
. See for instance
[
24
, Lemma 2.3]
, we could also reproduce the proof of Lemma
2.8
and use Corollary
5.2
. By Proposition
5.13
, there are a
\xi\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}
and a point
b^{\prime}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{2}
such that

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}\underset{t\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}b_{\infty},

while staying in
B_{2}
at all non-negative time. Let
\lambda_{1}<\cdots<\lambda_{m}
be the increasing eigenvalues of
\xi
and

G_{k}=\ker(\lambda_{k}\mathrm{Id}_{E}-\xi),\qquad F_{k}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}G_{i},

the associated eigenspaces. By Proposition
5.3
, each
F_{k}
is an admissible sub-bundle of
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
so, by local
P_{\zeta}
-polystability,
P_{\zeta}(F_{k})\leqslant 0
and in case of equality,
F_{k}^{\bot}
is admissible in
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
. Still by Proposition
5.3
, we have an orthogonal and holomorphic decomposition

\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{m}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}}).

(22)

Let for all
k
,
\Pi_{k}
be the orthogonal projection onto
G_{k}
. We have

\displaystyle\left<\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left<\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\lambda_{k}\Pi_{k}\right>

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}-2\pi\lambda_{k}\int_{X}\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{b_{\infty}},h))\textrm{ by (\ref{EQ:Décomposition E_binfty 2}),}

\displaystyle=-2\pi\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda_{k}P_{\zeta}(G_{k})

\displaystyle=-2\pi\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k+1})P_{\zeta}(F_{k})\textrm{ because }P_{\zeta}(E)=0,

\displaystyle\leqslant 0\textrm{ by local $P_{\zeta}$-semi-stability of $\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}$.}

Moreover, this quantity vanishes because
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=0
. We deduce that for all
k
,
P_{\zeta}(F_{k})=0
. By local
P_{\zeta}
-polystability of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
(thus of
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
), each
(F_{k}^{\bot},\overline{\partial}_{b^{\prime}})
is a holomorphic sub-bundle of
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
. Therefore, we have an orthogonal and holomorphic decomposition

\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{m}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{b^{\prime}}).

In particular, we deduce that
\xi
is
\overline{\partial}_{b^{\prime}}
-holomorphic so it stabilises
b^{\prime}
hence
b_{\infty}=b^{\prime}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{2}
. We deduce that
\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b}
is a solution to the
P_{\zeta}
-critical equation and by Proposition
5.6
, it belongs to
\mathcal{B}_{d}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-polystable in
\mathcal{B}_{d}
.

6.3
Equivalence of stabilities

We prove here the equivalence of Theorem
4.12
about the different notions of stabilities, still under the assumptions that the flows
\phi_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
exist in
B_{2}
at all non-negative time and converge in
B_{2}
.

Since we have already proven the equivalence between polystabilities in Sub-section
6.2
, all we have left to prove is that
\mathrm{Stab}(b)
is trivial if and only if
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is simple. It follows from Corollary
5.2
.

6.4
Uniqueness of solutions and Jordan–Hölder filtration

We show here a uniqueness result about solutions to the
P
-critical equation in each
\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)
-orbit. The next lemma is a standard GIT result, analogous to
[
24
, Lemma 2.1]
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Any two zeroes of
\mu_{\zeta}
(resp.
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}
) in the same
G
-orbit in
B_{3,0}
are in the same
K
-orbit.

Proof.

The proof is the same for
\mu_{\zeta}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}
. We prove it for
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}
. Let
b_{2}=u\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{\xi}\cdot b_{1}
with
u\in K
and
\xi\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}
be zeroes of
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}
in
B_{3,0}
. By Lemma
5.4
, all
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b_{1}
lie in
B_{4,0}
for
0\leqslant t\leqslant 1
. Let

a:t\mapsto\left<\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b_{1}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>.

We have
a(0)=a(1)=0
because
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{1})=\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{2})=0
and for all
t
,

a^{\prime}(t)=\left<d\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b_{1})L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b_{1}}\xi,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\xi\right>=\tilde{\Omega}_{\zeta,\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b_{1}}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b_{1}}\xi,L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b_{1}}\xi)\leqslant 0,

with equality if and only if
L_{\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b_{1}}\xi=0
. We deduce that
a
is non-increasing. But
a(0)=a(1)=0
so it must be constant. In particular,
a^{\prime}(0)=0
hence
L_{b_{1}}\xi=0
, which means that
b_{2}=u\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{\xi}\cdot b_{1}=u\cdot b_{1}\in K\cdot b_{1}
.
∎

Theorem 6.2

(Local uniqueness of solutions modulo
\mathcal{G}(E,h)
)
.

Let
(\zeta,b)\in\mathcal{U}_{d}\times B_{2}
. Two zeroes of
\mu_{\infty,\zeta}
in
\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b}\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}
are in the same
\mathcal{G}(E,h)
-orbit.

Proof.

Assume that
\overline{\partial}_{1}
and
\overline{\partial}_{2}
are in
\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b}\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}
and are
P_{\zeta}
-critical. By Proposition
5.6
, there are
b_{1}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{3,0}
,
b_{2}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{3,0}
,
u_{1}\in\mathcal{G}(E,h)
and
u_{2}\in\mathcal{G}(E,h)
such that
\overline{\partial}_{1}=u_{1}\cdot(\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,b_{1}))
and
\overline{\partial}_{2}=u_{2}\cdot(\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,b_{2}))
. Moreover,
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{1})=\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{2})=0
.

By Lemma
6.1
, there is a
v\in K
such that
b_{2}=v\cdot b_{1}
so, by
K
-invariance of
\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,\cdot)
,
\overline{\partial}_{2}=u_{2}vu_{1}^{\dagger}\cdot\overline{\partial}_{1}
.
∎

We now show the existence of admissible Jordan–Hölder filtrations and the existence and uniqueness of the associated graded object in the semi-stable case. We then deduce informations about the
K
-orbits of the limit points of the flows in function of the
G
-orbits of the starting point.

Theorem 6.3

(Jordan–Hölder filtration)
.

Up to shrinking
\mathcal{U}_{d}
and
B_{1}
, for all
b\in B_{1}
such that
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stable, there is a filtration of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
by admissible sub-bundles

0=F_{0}\subsetneq F_{1}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq F_{m}=E,

such that for all
k
,
P_{\zeta}(F_{k})=0
and each
\mathcal{G}_{k}=(F_{k},\overline{\partial}_{b})/(F_{k-1},\overline{\partial}_{b})=(F_{k}\cap F_{k-1},\overline{\partial}_{b})
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-stable.

Moreover, the graded object
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathcal{E}_{b})=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{G}_{k}
is unique up to an isomorphism in
G
, and is isomorphic to both
\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}
where
b_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
and
\tilde{b}_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
.

We prove this theorem under the assumption that the flows exist at all time and converge.

Lemma 6.4
.

Theorem
6.3
holds for
(\zeta,b)
as long as the flows
\phi_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
exist at all time and converge in
B_{2}
.

Proof.

Let us start with the existence. If
F_{1}\subset F_{2}\subset E
are admissible sub-bundles of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
with
\mathrm{rk}(F_{1})=\mathrm{rk}(F_{2})
, then
(F_{1}^{\bot},\overline{\partial}_{0})
and
(F_{2},\overline{\partial}_{0})
are sub-bundles of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
. We deduce that
(F_{1}^{\bot}\cap F_{2},\overline{\partial}_{0})
is a sub-sheaf of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
of rank
0
hence it is trivial because
\mathcal{E}_{0}
is torsion-free. It proves that in any filtration by admissible sub-bundles of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
, the ranks of the
F_{k}
increase.

Therefore, all such filtration has at most
\mathrm{rk}(E)
elements. To prove the existence of a Jordan–Hölder filtration by admissible sub-bundles, we can simply take a filtration by admissible sub-bundles
F_{k}
such that
P_{\zeta}(F_{k})=0
with a maximal number of elements and clearly, the quotients are locally
P_{\zeta}
-stable.

For the uniqueness of the graded object, the proof is the same as for
[
27
, Proposition 1.5.2]
. It relies on the fact that all maps between locally
P_{\zeta}
-stable bundles verifying
P_{\zeta}=0
is either
0
or an isomorphism, which is true thanks to Lemma
2.8
, with the same proof that for Proposition
2.10
.

Then, let
b_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
and
\tilde{b}_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
. We show that
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}
is isomorphic to the graded object because the proof is the same as for
\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
. By Sub-section
6.1
, local
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stability of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
implies that
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})=0
. Thus, all holomorphic sections of
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}
are parallel so by Proposition
5.13
, there are a
b^{\prime}\in G\cap b
and a
\xi\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}
such that

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}\underset{t\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

Let
(G_{k})
be the eigenspaces of
\xi
and
(F_{k})
the associated filtration by admissible sub-bundles of
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
. By Proposition
5.3
,

\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{m}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}).

Moreover, by Sub-section
6.2
,
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-polystable. We deduce that
(F_{k})
is a Jordan–Hölder filtration of
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}
is isomorphic to its graded object. In particular, it is isomorphic to the graded object of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
.
∎

Corollary 6.5
.

Let
b
and
b^{\prime}
in
B_{2}
in the same
G
-orbit such that
\mathcal{E}_{b}
(thus
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
) is locally
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stable. Assume that the flows
\phi_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
,
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
,
\phi_{\zeta}(b^{\prime},\cdot)
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b^{\prime},\cdot)
exist at all time and converge in
B_{2}
. Let
b_{\infty}
,
\tilde{b}_{\infty}
,
b_{\infty}^{\prime}
and
\tilde{b}_{\infty}^{\prime}
their respective limits. Then,
b_{\infty}^{\prime}\in K\cdot b_{\infty}
,
\tilde{b}_{\infty}^{\prime}\in K\cdot\tilde{b}_{\infty}
and
\tilde{b}_{\infty}\in G\cdot b_{\infty}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.4
,
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
and the isomorphism between them lies in
G
thus
\tilde{b}_{\infty}\in G\cdot b_{\infty}
. The proof of the two other assumptions are the same so we prove that
\tilde{b}_{\infty}^{\prime}\in K\cdot\tilde{b}_{\infty}
. Indeed, since
\mathcal{E}_{b}\cong\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
with an isomorphism in
G
, their graded objects are isomorphic with an isomorphism in
G
because we can find isomorphic Jordan–Hölder filtrations. It implies that
\tilde{b}_{\infty}^{\prime}\in G\cdot\tilde{b}_{\infty}
.

We conclude thanks to Remark
6.1
.
∎

6.5
Existence of flows and end of proofs

We show in this sub-section the final argument to conclude the proof of Theorem
4.12

i.e.
the fact that the flows
\phi_{\zeta}
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}
are defined at all time for all
b
close enough to
0
and all
\zeta
close enough to
\zeta_{0}
.

Let
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}
and
b\in B_{2}
. Let
T\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\cup\{+\infty\}
the largest positive number such that
\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
is well-defined for all
t<T
. If
T<+\infty
, for all
t\geqslant T
, we set
\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)=\bot
where
\bot
means ”undefined”. If
T=+\infty
and
\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
stays in a compact subset of
B_{2}
, by Proposition
5.11
, we can set
\phi_{\zeta}(b,+\infty)=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
which exists in
B_{2}
. Finally, if
T=+\infty
but
\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
does not stay in a compact subset of
B_{2}
, we set
\phi_{\zeta}(b,+\infty)=\bot
.

We do the same thing with
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}
. We endow the set
B_{2}\cup\{\bot\}
with the topology that makes
\bot
an isolated point.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
b_{m}\rightarrow 0
and
\zeta_{m}\rightarrow\zeta_{0}
. Let
(t_{m})
be any sequence of
\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{+\infty\}
. We have
\phi_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m})\rightarrow 0
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m})\rightarrow 0
.

Proof.

The proof is the same for both flows so we only prove it for
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}
. Assume it is not the case so up to an extraction, there is an
\varepsilon>0
such that for all
m
,
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m})=\bot
or
\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m})\right\|_{L^{2}}>\varepsilon
. Let
r
be the radius of
B_{1}
and assume up to reducing
\varepsilon
that
\varepsilon<r
. Assume up to an extraction that for all
m
,
\left\|b_{m}\right\|\leqslant\varepsilon
. By continuity of
t\mapsto\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t)
as long as this flow is well-defined, there is a
t_{m}^{\prime}\in[0,t_{m}[
such that
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m}^{\prime})\neq\bot
and

\left\|\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m}^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{2}}=\varepsilon.

In particular,
t_{m}^{\prime}<+\infty
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m}^{\prime})\in B_{1}
. By compactness of the sphere, we may also assume up to an extraction that
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m}^{\prime})\rightarrow b_{\infty}\in B_{1}
.

By Lemma
5.8
,
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m}^{\prime})\in G\cdot b_{m}
. Moreover, by Propositions
5.10
and
5.11
,
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{0}}(b,t)
is well-defined in
B_{2}
for all
b\in B_{1}
and all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{+\infty\}
. Moreover,
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{0}}(b,+\infty)
is a zero of
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta_{0}}
. Therefore, we may apply Corollary
6.5
to deduce that

\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{0}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m}^{\prime}),+\infty)=u_{m}\cdot\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{0}}(b_{m},+\infty)\in B_{2}

(23)

for some
u_{m}\in K
. Moreover, by Proposition
5.12
,
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{0}}(\cdot,+\infty)
is continuous at
0
and
b_{\infty}
thus, when we take the limit of (
23
), we obtain

\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{0}}(b_{\infty},+\infty)=0.

(24)

Moreover, for all
m
, since
\tilde{f}_{\zeta_{m}}
decreases along the flow lines,

\tilde{f}_{\zeta_{m}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m}^{\prime}))\leqslant\tilde{f}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m})\rightarrow\tilde{f}_{\zeta_{0}}(0)=0.

We deduce that

\tilde{f}_{\zeta_{0}}(b_{\infty})=\lim_{m\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{f}_{\zeta_{m}}(\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m}^{\prime}))=0.

(25)

Combining (
24
) and (
25
), we deduce that
b_{\infty}=0
, hence a contradiction.
∎

Corollary 6.7
.

Up to shrinking
\mathcal{U}_{d}
and
B_{1}
, the flows
\phi_{\zeta}
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}
exist at all time and stay in a compact subset
K
of
B_{2}
that we can choose independent of
\zeta
and
b
.

Proof.

Lemma
6.6
shows that the functions
(\zeta,b,t)\mapsto\phi_{\zeta}(b,t),\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
are continuous at each point of
\{(\zeta_{0},0)\}\times(\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{+\infty\})
. Let
B\subsetneq B_{2}
be any smaller ball so
K=\overline{B}
is compact in
B_{2}
.

By continuity, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{+\infty\}
, there is an open neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}_{d,s}
of
\zeta_{0}
in
\mathcal{U}_{d}
, an open ball
B_{1,s}
around
0
and an open neighbourhood
I_{s}
of
s
in
\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{+\infty\}
such that
\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
both lie in
B\subset K
when
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d,s}
,
b\in B_{1,s}
and
t\in I_{s}
. By compactness of
\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{+\infty\}
, there is a finite family
s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}
of elements of this set such that
\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{+\infty\}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{m}I_{s_{k}}
.

Up to shrinking
\mathcal{U}_{d}
and
B_{1}
, we may assume that

\mathcal{U}_{d}\subset\bigcap_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{U}_{d,s_{k}},\qquad B_{1}\subset\bigcap_{k=1}^{m}B_{1,s_{k}}.

It proves the corollary.
∎

Corollary 6.8
.

Theorems
4.12
and
6.3
are true.

Proof.

It follows from Sub-sections
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.3
and Corollary
6.7
for Theorem
4.12
. It follows from Lemma
6.4
and Corollary
6.7
for Theorem
6.3
.
∎

Then, we prove Theorem
4.13
and Proposition
4.14
.

Proof of Theorem
4.13
.

Let
b\in B_{1}
. By Propositions
5.10
and
5.11
and (
13
),
b_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta_{0}}(b,t)\in\overline{G\cdot b}\cap B_{2}
is a zero of
\mu_{\zeta_{0}}
, which proves the
\mu_{\zeta_{0}}
-semi-stability of
b
.

If
G\cdot b
is closed, then
b_{\infty}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{2}
so
b
is
\mu_{\zeta_{0}}
-polystable. Reciprocally, assume that
b
is
\mu_{\zeta_{0}}
-polystable. Let
F\subset E
such that
(F,\overline{\partial}_{0})\subset\mathcal{E}_{0}
and
(F^{\bot},\overline{\partial}_{0})\subset\mathcal{E}_{0}
are sub-bundles. Let
\xi=-\mathrm{rk}(F^{\bot})\Pi_{F}+\mathrm{rk}(F)\Pi_{F^{\bot}}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}
, where
\Pi_{F}
is the orthogonal projection onto
F
and
\Pi_{F^{\bot}}=\mathrm{Id}_{E}-\Pi_{F}
is the orthogonal projection onto
F^{\bot}
. Let
b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{*}b\cap B_{1}
. If
(F,\overline{\partial}_{b})\subset\mathcal{E}_{b}
, then by Proposition
5.3
,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b
remains in
B_{1}
for all
t
and converges in it with a decreasing norm. Since the action of
G
on
V
is linear,
\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{t\xi}\cdot b^{\prime}
remains in
B_{1}
and converges in it with a decreasing norm. By Proposition
5.3
, it implies that
(F,\overline{\partial}_{b^{\prime}})\subset\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
is holomorphic.

We just showed that all admissible sub-bundles of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
are admissible sub-bundles of
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
is locally
P_{\zeta_{0}}
-polystable. Notice that
b^{\prime}
need not be in the gauge orbit of
b
. Let
b_{0}\in\overline{G\cdot b}
. Still by linearity of the action, for all
\varepsilon>0
,
\varepsilon b_{0}\in\overline{G\cdot(\varepsilon b)}
. Choose
\varepsilon
small enough so
\varepsilon b
and
\varepsilon b_{0}
both lie in
B_{1}
. As we saw previously,
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon b}
is locally
P_{\zeta_{0}}
-polystable.

Let
b_{\infty,\varepsilon}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta_{0}}(\varepsilon b,t)\in B_{2}
. By Sub-section
6.2
,
b_{\infty,\varepsilon}\in G\cdot(\varepsilon b)
is a zero of
\mu_{\zeta_{0}}
. Moreover, by Corollary
6.5
, for all
b^{\prime}\in G\cdot(\varepsilon b)\cap B_{1}
,
\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta_{0}}(b^{\prime},t)\in K\cdot b_{\infty,\varepsilon}
. Moreover, by closeness of
K
-orbits and Proposition
5.12
, it is also true for
b^{\prime}\in\overline{G\cdot(\varepsilon b)}\cap B_{1}
. In particular, for
b^{\prime}=\varepsilon b_{0}
,
\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta_{0}}(\varepsilon b_{0},t)\in K\cdot b_{\infty,\varepsilon}
.

In particular,
\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta_{0}}(\varepsilon b_{0},t)\in G\cdot(\varepsilon b)
, which implies that
\varepsilon b\in\overline{G\cdot(\varepsilon b_{0})}
. Since
\varepsilon b_{0}\in\overline{G\cdot(\varepsilon b)}
, usual results about actions of reductive Lie groups imply that
\varepsilon b_{0}\in G\cdot(\varepsilon b)
and by linearity of the action,
b_{0}\in G\cdot b
proving that this orbit is closed.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.14
.

By Theorem
4.12
, we can replace ”
\mu_{\zeta}(b)
-(semi-)(poly)stable in
B_{2}
” by ”locally
P_{\zeta}
-(semi-)(poly)stable” in the definitions of
L_{\mathrm{s}}
,
L_{\mathrm{ps}}
and
L_{\mathrm{ss}}
. From here, the belonging of
(\zeta,b)
to any of those sets only depend on
[\zeta]
and
G\cdot b\cap B_{1}
because for any
b^{\prime}\in G\cdot b\cap B_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}_{b^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{E}_{b}
with an isomorphism in
G
(so they have the same admissible sub-bundles) and for any bundle
F
,
P_{\zeta}(F)
only depends on
[\zeta]
.

Claim 1 :

L_{\mathrm{s}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{d}\times B_{1}
is open.

Let
(\zeta_{1},b_{1})\in L_{\mathrm{s}}
and
g\in G
such that
g\cdot b_{1}\in B_{2}
and
\mu_{\zeta_{1}}(g\cdot b_{1})=0
. Let

\Psi:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{d}\times B_{1}\times\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}\\
\displaystyle(\zeta,b,s)&\longmapsto&\displaystyle\mu_{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{s}g\cdot b)\end{array}\right..

We have for all
(v,w)\in\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\mathfrak{k}\times\mathfrak{k}
,

\displaystyle\left<\frac{\partial}{\partial s}|_{s=0}\Psi(\zeta_{1},b_{1},s)v,w\right>

\displaystyle=\left<d\mu_{\zeta_{1}}(g\cdot b_{1})L_{g\cdot b_{1}}v,w\right>

\displaystyle=\Omega_{\zeta_{1},g\cdot b_{1}}(L_{g\cdot b_{1}}w,L_{g\cdot b_{1}}v).

By assumption,
\mathrm{Stab}(g\cdot b_{1})=\mathrm{Stab}(b_{1})=\{\mathrm{Id}_{E}\}
so
L_{g\cdot b_{1}}:\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow T_{g\cdot b_{1}}B_{2}
is injective. Since
\Omega_{\zeta_{1}}
is a positive form on
B_{2}
, then
\left<\frac{\partial}{\partial s}|_{s=0}\Psi(\zeta_{1},b_{1},s)v,w\right>=0
for all
w
if and only if
v=0
. It shows the injectivity, thus the bijectivity by equality of dimensions, of the linearisation of
\Psi
at
(\zeta_{1},b_{1},0)
with respect to the third variable. We easily conclude with the implicit functions theorem.

Claim 2 :
For all fixed
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}
,
\{b\in B_{1}|(\zeta,b)\in L_{\mathrm{ss}}\subset B_{1}
is open.

Let
(\zeta_{1},b_{1})\in L_{\mathrm{ss}}
. Let
b_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta_{1}}(b_{1},t)
, which is a zero of
\mu_{\zeta_{1}}
. By applying Proposition
5.9
, we see that in an open neighbourhood
U\subset B_{2}
of
b_{\infty}
, all the critical points of
f_{\zeta_{1}}=\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mu_{\zeta_{1}}\right\|^{2}
are zeroes of this function.

By Proposition
5.12
, if
b
is close enough to
b_{1}
,
\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta_{1}}(b,t)\in U
and it is a critical point of
f_{\zeta_{1}}
thus a zero of
\mu_{\zeta_{1}}
, proving the semi-stability of such a
b
.

Claim 3 :
For all fixed
b\in B_{1}
,
\{\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}|(\zeta,b)\in L_{\mathrm{ss}}\}\subset\mathcal{U}_{d}
is a closed polyhedral cone centred at
0
defined by a finite number of inequalities on
[\zeta-\zeta_{0}]
.

It is due to the fact that the number of admissible sub-bundles of each
\mathcal{E}_{b}
up to diffeomorphism is finite and the inequalities
P_{\zeta}(F)\leqslant 0
are linear in
[\zeta-\zeta_{0}]
.

Claim 4 :

\{\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}|(\zeta,b)\in L_{\mathrm{s}}\}\subset\mathcal{U}_{d}
is its interior.

It is a standard convex geometry result that the interior of a closed polyhedral cone defined by some linear inequalities
l_{1}(x),\ldots,l_{m}(x)\leqslant 0
is the polyhedral cone defined by the inequalities
l_{1}(x),\ldots,l_{m}(x)<0
. Here, local
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stability of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is defined by
P_{\zeta}(F)\leqslant 0
for all
F\subset E
admissible in
\mathcal{E}_{b}
and local
P_{\zeta}
-stability of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is defined by
P_{\zeta}(F)<0
for all
F\subset E
admissible in
\mathcal{E}_{b}
, hence the result.

Claim 5 :
For all fixed
b\in B_{1}
, if
(\zeta_{0},b)\in L_{\mathrm{ps}}
,
\{\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}|(\zeta,b)\in L_{\mathrm{ps}}\}=\{\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}|(\zeta,b)\in L_{\mathrm{ss}}\}
.

It is a consequence of the fact that if
(\zeta_{0},b)\in L_{\mathrm{ps}}
, for all admissible sub-bundles
F\subset E
of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
, we have
P_{\zeta_{0}}(F)=0
so
(F,\overline{\partial}_{b})
destabilises
\mathcal{E}_{b}
at
\zeta_{0}
thus
F^{\bot}
is also admissible in
\mathcal{E}_{b}
. And for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}
,
P_{\zeta}(F^{\bot})=-P_{\zeta}(F)
.

Claim 6 :
For all fixed
b\in B_{1}
, if
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is simple,
\{\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}|(\zeta,0)\in L_{\mathrm{ps}}\}=\{\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}|(\zeta,0)\in L_{\mathrm{s}}\}
.

Trivial from the definition of local
P_{\zeta}
-stability.
∎

To conclude this section, we show a result which is analogous to the second Ness uniqueness theorem
[
24
, Theorem 6.5]
in the semi-stable case.

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
b\in B_{1}
and
b_{\infty}\in\overline{G\cdot b}\cap B_{1}
a zero of
\mu_{\zeta}
or
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}
. Then,
\mathcal{E}_{b}
is locally
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stable and
\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
is isomorphic to the graded object of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
.

Proof.

The local
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stability of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
comes from Proposition
4.14
. Assume without loss of generality that
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=0
. Let
b_{m}\rightarrow b_{\infty}
a sequence of points of
G\cdot b\cap B_{1}
. Let
b_{\infty}^{\prime}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
. By Corollary
6.5
, for all
m
,

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b_{m},t)\in K\cdot b_{\infty}^{\prime}.

By Proposition
5.12
and closeness of
K
-orbits, when
m\rightarrow+\infty
,

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty},t)\in K\cdot b_{\infty}^{\prime}.

Since
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})=0
, we deduce that
b_{\infty}\in K\cdot b_{\infty}^{\prime}
and by Theorem
6.3
,
\mathrm{Gr}(\mathcal{E}_{b})\cong\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
.
∎

7
Harder–Narasimhan filtrations and a local Kempf–Ness theorem

7.1
Harder–Narasimhan filtrations

We build a local version of the Harder–Narasimhan filtration. We also show a link between this filtration and the limit points of the flows. For this, we need to be able to apply our previous result to all the destabilising sub-bundles of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{0}=(F_{0},\overline{\partial}_{0})\subset\mathcal{E}_{0}
be a non-zero direct sum of the simple components
\mathcal{G}_{0,k}
of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
. In particular, we have an orthogonal and holomorphic decomposition

\mathcal{E}_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{0}/\mathcal{F}_{0}

and the smooth structure of
\mathcal{E}_{0}/\mathcal{F}_{0}
is
F_{0}^{\bot}
. It induces an
L^{2}
-orthogonal decomposition of
V
,

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{E}_{0}))

\displaystyle=H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{F}_{0}))\oplus H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{E}_{0}/\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0}))

\displaystyle\oplus H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{0}/\mathcal{F}_{0}))\oplus H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{E}_{0}/\mathcal{F}_{0})).

Let
W=H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{F}_{0}))
so
W^{\bot}=H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{E}_{0}/\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0}))\oplus H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{0}/\mathcal{F}_{0}))\oplus H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{E}_{0}/\mathcal{F}_{0}))
. When
\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}
(recall that it means that
\zeta
is closed and
P_{\zeta}(E)=0
), we define

\zeta^{F_{0}}=\zeta-\frac{P_{\zeta}(F_{0})}{\mathrm{rk}(F_{0})}\frac{\mathrm{Vol}}{\mathrm{Vol}(X)}.

We have that
P_{\zeta^{F_{0}}}(F_{0})=0
and the map
\zeta\mapsto\zeta^{F_{0}}
is linear and sends
\zeta_{0}
onto itself.

There is a finite number of such sub-bundles up to an isomorphism in
G
because they are all isomorphic to direct sums of some
\mathcal{G}_{0,k}
. Therefore, if
B_{2}
is small enough, we may assume, up to shrinking
\mathcal{U}_{d}
and
B_{1}
that for all
(\zeta,b)\in\mathcal{U}_{d}\times(B_{1}\cap W)
, all the results of the previous sections apply to
\mathcal{F}_{0}
with
(\zeta^{F_{0}},b)
.

Theorem 7.1

(Harder–Narasimhan filtration)
.

For all
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}
and
b\in B_{1}
, there is a unique filtration of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
by admissible sub-bundles

0=F_{0}\subsetneq F_{1}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq F_{m}=E,

such that for all
k
, each
\mathcal{G}_{k}=(F_{k},\overline{\partial}_{b})/(F_{k-1},\overline{\partial}_{b})=(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{b})
(where
G_{k}=F_{k}\cap F_{k-1}^{\bot}
) is locally
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stable and
\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{m})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{m})}<\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{m-1})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{m-1})}<\cdots<\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{1})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{1})}
.

Moreover, if we set
b_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
,
\tilde{b}_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
and
\Pi_{k}
the orthogonal projection onto
G_{k}
, then, there are
u
and
\tilde{u}
in
K
such that

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}u\mu_{\zeta}(b_{\infty})u^{\dagger}=\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{u}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})\tilde{u}^{\dagger}=-\frac{2\pi}{\mathrm{Vol}(X)}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{k})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{k})}\Pi_{k}

and the direct sum of the graded objects of the semi-stable quotients
\bigoplus_{k=1}^{m}\mathrm{Gr}(\mathcal{G}_{k})
is isomorphic to both
\mathcal{E}_{b_{\infty}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}
.

Proof.

For the uniqueness, the proof is the same as for
[
27
, Theorem 1.3.4]
. We could also show the existence the same way, but we will directly prove it thanks to the flows. We prove the statement of the theorem for
\tilde{b}_{\infty}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
since the proof for the limit point of the other flow is the same.

Let
\lambda_{1}<\cdots<\lambda_{m}
be the distinct eigenvalues of
-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})
and

G_{k}=\ker(\lambda_{k}\mathrm{Id}_{E}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})),\qquad F_{k}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}G_{i},

the associated eigenspaces. Let for all
k
,
\Pi_{k}
be the orthogonal projection onto
G_{k}
so we have

-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda_{k}\Pi_{k}.

By Proposition
5.11
,
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})
is
\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}
-holomorphic so we have an orthogonal and holomorphic decomposition

\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{m}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}}).

We deduce that for all
k
, on the one hand

\left<\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\Pi_{k}\right>=-2\pi\int_{X}\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}},h))=-2\pi P_{\zeta}(G_{k}),

and on the other hand

\left<\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\Pi_{k}\right>=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}\left<\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\Pi_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\Pi_{k}\right>=\lambda_{k}\left\|\Pi_{k}\right\|^{2}=\lambda_{k}\mathrm{rk}(G_{k})\mathrm{Vol}(X).

Thus
\lambda_{k}=-\frac{2\pi}{\mathrm{Vol}(X)}\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{k})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{k})}
. In particular,
\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{k})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{k})}
decreases with
k
and

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})=-\frac{2\pi}{\mathrm{Vol}(X)}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{k})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{k})}\Pi_{k}.

Then, by Proposition
5.14
, there is a sequence
\tilde{b}_{i}\in G\cdot b
which converges toward
\tilde{b}_{\infty}
and such that
t\mapsto\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{-t\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})}\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}
converges in
B_{2}
. Let for all
i
,
\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{-t\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})}\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}\in B_{2}
.

The map
b^{\prime}\mapsto\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{-t\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})}\cdot b^{\prime}
is linear where it is defined as a limit of linear actions. In particular, it is continuous and since it fixes
\tilde{b}_{\infty}
(because
\tilde{b}_{\infty}
is a critical point of
\frac{1}{2}\left\|\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}\right\|^{2}
), we have
\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}\rightarrow\tilde{b}_{\infty}
.

The fact that
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})
acts trivially on each
\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}
implies that each
\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}
is block diagonal in the orthogonal decomposition
E=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{m}G_{k}
and each
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}}
decomposes as an orthogonal and holomorphic direct sum

\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{m}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}}).

When looking at each block, we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}),h)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\pi}\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,\tilde{b}_{\infty,i})_{|\mathrm{End}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{0})}\,\mathrm{Vol}

\displaystyle\underset{i\rightarrow+\infty}{\longrightarrow}-\frac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\pi}\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,\tilde{b}_{\infty})_{|\mathrm{End}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{0})}\,\mathrm{Vol}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\lambda_{k}\mathrm{Id}_{G_{k}}\mathrm{Vol}

\displaystyle=\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{k})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{k})}\mathrm{Id}_{G_{k}}\frac{\mathrm{Vol}}{\mathrm{Vol}(X)}.

We deduce that
\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,\tilde{b}_{\infty,i})
converges toward a
P_{\zeta^{G_{k}}}
-critical operator where we recall that
\zeta^{G_{k}}=\zeta-\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{k})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{k})}\frac{\mathrm{Vol}}{\mathrm{Vol}(X)}\mathrm{Id}_{G_{k}}
.

By Proposition
4.14
, being
\mu_{\infty,\zeta^{G_{k}}}
-semi-stable in
\mathcal{B}_{d}
is an open condition so if
i
is large enough,
(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}})
is
\mu_{\infty,\zeta^{G_{k}}}
-semi-stable in
\mathcal{B}_{d}
. By Theorem
4.12
, it is locally
P_{\zeta^{G_{k}}}
-semi-stable. We easily see that it is equivalent to being locally
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stable.

Moreover, by Proposition
5.3
, the existence of
\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}t\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})}\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}
implies that the filtration

0=F_{0}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq F_{m}=E

is a filtration by admissible sub-bundles in
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{i}}
and

(F_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{i}})\cap(F_{k-1},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{i}})=(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}})

is locally
P_{\zeta}
-semi-stable. Moreover, as the
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{i}}
are all isomorphic (with isomorphisms in
G
) and by uniqueness of the Harder–Narasimhan filtration, the filtrations
(F_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{i}})
are isomorphic. In particular, the
(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}})
are isomorphic and they approach
(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}})
which is locally
P_{\zeta^{G_{k}}}
-polystable. We may apply Proposition
6.9
to deduce that

(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty}})=\mathrm{Gr}(G_{k},\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{b}_{\infty,i}}).

Let
i
be any integer. We have
b\in G\cdot\tilde{b}_{i}
so the Harder–Narasimhan filtration of
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{b}_{i}}
yields a Harder–Narasimhan filtration
(F_{k}^{\prime})_{1\leqslant k\leqslant m}
of
\mathcal{E}_{b}
which is isomorphic to the previous one. In particular, we can build a unitary
\tilde{u}\in K
such that for all
k
,
F_{k}^{\prime}=\tilde{u}(F_{k})
. The orthogonal projection onto
G_{k}^{\prime}=F_{k}^{\prime}\cap{F_{k-1}^{\prime}}^{\bot}
is
\Pi_{k}^{\prime}=\tilde{u}\Pi_{k}\tilde{u}^{\dagger}
so

-\frac{2\pi}{\mathrm{Vol}(X)}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{k}^{\prime})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{k}^{\prime})}\Pi_{k}^{\prime}=-\frac{2\pi}{\mathrm{Vol}(X)}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{P_{\zeta}(G_{k})}{\mathrm{rk}(G_{k})}\tilde{u}\Pi_{k}\tilde{u}^{\dagger}=-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\tilde{u}\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}(\tilde{b}_{\infty})\tilde{u}^{\dagger}.

∎

7.2
A local Kempf–Ness theorem and continuity results

We end up this section by showing the continuity results about the solutions to the
P
-critical equation and a local version of the Kempf–Ness theorem. Recall that

L_{\mathrm{ss}}=\{(\zeta,b)\in\mathcal{U}_{d}\times B_{1}|b\textrm{ is $\mu_{\zeta}$-semi-stable in }B_{2}\}.

Theorem 7.2

(Continuity of solutions of the
P
-critical equation modulo unitary transforms)
.

Up to shrinking
\mathcal{U}_{d}
and
B_{1}
, the maps

\Gamma:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{ss}}&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle B_{2}/K\\
\displaystyle(\zeta,b)&\longmapsto&\displaystyle K\cdot\left(\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)\right)\end{array}\right.,

\Gamma_{\infty}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{ss}}&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{d}/\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))\\
\displaystyle(\zeta,b)&\longmapsto&\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t))\right)\end{array}\right.

are well-defined,
G
-invariant and continuous.

For each
(\zeta,b)
,
\Gamma(\zeta,b)
is the orbit of a zero of
\mu_{\zeta}
in
\overline{G\cdot b}\cap B_{2}
and
\Gamma_{\infty}(\zeta,b)
is the orbit of a
P_{\zeta}
-critical operator in
\overline{\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b}}\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}
. Moreover, if
(\zeta,b)\in L_{\mathrm{ps}}
,
\Gamma(\zeta,b)\subset G\cdot b
and
\Gamma_{\infty}(\zeta,b)\subset\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b}
.

Proof.

\Gamma
and
\tilde{\Gamma}
are well-defined by Corollary
6.7
and they are
G
-invariant by Corollary
6.5
. By the results of Sub-sections
6.1
and
6.2
, the second part of the theorem is true. All we have left to show is their continuity.

By Corollary
6.7
applied to
B_{1}\subset B_{2,0}
, up to shrinking
\mathcal{U}_{d}
, there is a ball
B_{1}^{\prime}\subset B_{1}
such that for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}
, the flows
\phi_{\zeta}
and
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}
are well-defined and converge in a compact subset of
B_{1}
(independent of
\zeta
and
b
) as long as the starting point is in
B_{1}^{\prime}
. Let us show the continuity of
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{\infty}
on
L_{\mathrm{ss}}\cap(\mathcal{U}_{d}\times B_{1}^{\prime})
. Let

\tilde{\Gamma}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{ss}}&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle B_{2}/K\\
\displaystyle(\zeta,b)&\longmapsto&\displaystyle K\cdot\left(\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)\right)\end{array}\right..

By continuity of
\tilde{\Phi}
(see Proposition
4.5
), the continuity of
\tilde{\Gamma}
at some point implies the continuity of
\Gamma_{\infty}
at the same point. The proof of the continuity of
\Gamma
is the same as the proof of the continuity of
\tilde{\Gamma}
so we only do it for
\tilde{\Gamma}
.

Let
(\zeta_{1},b_{1})\in L_{\mathrm{ss}}
with
b_{1}\in B_{1}^{\prime}
and
K\cdot b_{\infty}=\tilde{\Gamma}(\zeta_{1},b_{1})
which is in
B_{1}/K
by construction of
B_{1}^{\prime}
. Let
(\zeta_{m},b_{m})
be a sequence of elements of
L_{\mathrm{ss}}
that converges toward
(\zeta_{1},b_{1})
. Since the
\tilde{\Gamma}(\zeta_{m},b_{m})
stay in a compact subset of
B_{1}
independent of
m
, it is enough to show that
K\cdot b_{\infty}
is its only limit up to an extraction. Let
K\cdot b_{\infty}^{\prime}
with
b_{\infty}^{\prime}\in B_{1}
be a limit of this sequence up to an extraction.

Set for all
m
,
b_{\infty,m}=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t)\in B_{1}
. Up to an extraction,
(b_{\infty,m})
converges in
\overline{B_{1}}
and its limit must lie in
K\cdot b_{\infty}^{\prime}
. Up to replacing
b_{\infty}^{\prime}
by an element of its
K
-orbit, we may assume that
b_{\infty,m}\rightarrow b_{\infty}^{\prime}
. Let for all
m
,
b_{m}^{\prime}=\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{m}}(b_{m},t_{m})
with
t_{m}<+\infty
chosen large enough so the distance between
b_{m}^{\prime}
and
b_{\infty,m}
converges toward
0
. In particular,
b_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow b_{\infty}^{\prime}
so for
m
large enough,
b_{m}^{\prime}\in B_{1}
.

We then apply the same trick as in the proof of Lemma
6.6
. By Lemma
5.8
,
b_{m}^{\prime}\in G\cdot b_{m}\cap B_{1}
. Therefore, by Corollary
6.5
,

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{1}}(b_{m}^{\prime},t)=u_{m}\cdot\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{1}}(b_{m},t)

(26)

for some
u_{m}\in K
. Up to an extraction, we may assume that
u_{m}\rightarrow u_{\infty}\in K
. Moreover, by Proposition
5.12
,
\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{1}}(\cdot,t)
is continuous at
b_{1}
and
b_{\infty}^{\prime}
thus, when we take the limit of (
26
), we obtain

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{1}}(b_{\infty}^{\prime},t)=u_{\infty}\cdot\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{1}}(b_{1},t)\in K\cdot b_{\infty}.

(27)

Moreover, for all
m
,
b_{\infty,m}
is a zero of
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta_{m}}
by the semi-stability assumption. Therefore,
b_{\infty}^{\prime}=\lim_{m\rightarrow+\infty}b_{\infty,m}
is a zero of
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta_{1}}
. It implies that

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta_{1}}(b_{\infty}^{\prime},t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}b_{\infty}^{\prime}=b_{\infty}^{\prime}.

(28)

Combining (
27
) and (
28
), we obtain that
K\cdot b_{\infty}^{\prime}=K\cdot b_{\infty}
hence the wanted result.

Up to replacing
B_{1}
by
B_{1}^{\prime}
,
\Gamma
and
\tilde{\Gamma}
(thus
\Gamma_{\infty}
) are continuous on their domain.
∎

Remark
.

We expect the maps
(\zeta,b)\mapsto\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
and
(\zeta,b)\mapsto\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
to be continuous on the semi-stable locus even without quotienting by
K
. In this case, we would produce a family of
P_{\zeta}
-critical operators which is continuous with respect to
(\zeta,b)
. However, showing this continuity probably requires to study more deeply the
g(t)\in G
and
\tilde{g}(t)\in G
of Lemma
5.8
as in the proof of Proposition
5.14
, which is tedious.

Notice that we already have the wanted continuity result when
\zeta
is fixed by Proposition
5.12
.

The germ of action of
G
on
B_{1}
extends to
\mathcal{U}_{d}\times B_{1}
where
G
acts trivially on
\mathcal{U}_{d}
. Moreover, it preserves
L_{\mathrm{ss}}
. Let
q:L_{\mathrm{ss}}\rightarrow L_{\mathrm{ss}}/G
be the associated quotient map. Let for all
\zeta
, the polystable GIT quotient,

B_{1}/\!/\!_{\zeta}G=\{q(\zeta,b)|(\zeta,b)\in L_{\mathrm{ps}}\}\subset L_{\mathrm{ss}}/G.

\mathcal{B}_{d}^{\mathrm{int}}\subset\mathcal{B}_{d}
be the set of
\overline{\partial}\in\mathcal{B}_{d}
which are integrable
i.e.

\overline{\partial}^{2}=0
. Let
B_{1}^{\mathrm{int}}\subset B_{1}
and
B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}}\subset B_{2}
be the sets of
b\in B_{1}
(resp.
b\in B_{2}
) such that
\overline{\partial}_{b}
is integrable. By Proposition
4.3
, they are
G
-invariant analytic subsets of
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
(and clearly,
B_{1}^{\mathrm{int}}=B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}}\cap B_{1}
).

Since
B_{1}^{\mathrm{int}}
is
G
-invariant, we can define naturally

B_{1}^{\mathrm{int}}/\!/\!_{\zeta}G\subset B_{1}/\!/\!_{\zeta}G.

Lemma 7.3
.

For all
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}
, the map

\varphi_{\zeta}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle(\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2})/K&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}^{d}(\mu_{\infty,\zeta}^{-1}\{0\})\cap\mathcal{B}_{d})/\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))\\
\displaystyle b&\longmapsto&\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{0}+\tilde{\Phi}(\zeta,b)\end{array}\right.

is a homeomorphism into its image. Moreover, its restriction to
B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}}

\varphi_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}:(\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}})/K\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{d}(\mu_{\infty,\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}^{\mathrm{int}})/\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))

is also a homeomorphism into its image and it has an open image.

Proof.

It is well-defined and continuous by Proposition
4.5
. Let

\mathcal{I}=\left\{\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))\cdot\overline{\partial}\,\middle|\,\overline{\partial}\in\mathcal{B}_{d},\mu_{\infty,\zeta}(\overline{\partial})=0\textrm{ and }\exists b\in B_{2},\overline{\partial}\in\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b}\right\}.

By Propositions
4.5
and
5.6
,
\mathcal{I}
contains the image of
\varphi_{\zeta}
. Let
\beta:\mathcal{B}_{d}\rightarrow B_{3,0}
be the smooth function given by Proposition
5.6
. If
(\overline{\partial}_{1},\overline{\partial}_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{d}^{2}
are two representatives of a same
\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))
-orbit in
\mathcal{I}
, then by Propositions
5.6
and
4.5
,
\overline{\partial}_{1}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\beta(\overline{\partial}_{1})}
and
\overline{\partial}_{2}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\beta(\overline{\partial}_{2})}
. Thus,
\overline{\partial}_{\beta(\overline{\partial}_{2})}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\beta(\overline{\partial}_{1})}
. Moreover,
\beta(\overline{\partial}_{1})
and
\beta(\overline{\partial}_{2})
are zeroes of
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}
.

By Corollary
5.2
,
\beta(\overline{\partial}_{2})\in G\cdot\beta(\overline{\partial}_{1})
and by Lemma
6.1
,
\beta(\overline{\partial}_{2})\in K\cdot\beta(\overline{\partial}_{1})
. By the universal property of quotients,
\beta
factorises as a continuous map

\overline{\beta}:\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow(\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{3,0})/K.

Moreover, Proposition
5.6
and Lemma
6.1
imply that

\overline{\beta}\circ\varphi_{\zeta}:(\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2})/K\longrightarrow(\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{3,0})/K

is the identity map. Therefore,
\varphi_{\zeta}
is injective and is a homeomorphism into its image. It also shows that
\varphi_{\zeta}
is open in
\mathcal{I}
. Let

\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{int}}=\left\{\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))\cdot\overline{\partial}\in\mathcal{I}|\overline{\partial}^{2}=0\right\}.

By restriction,
\varphi_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}:(\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}})/K\rightarrow\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{int}}
is also an open homeomorphism into its image. Finally, by Proposition
4.3
,

\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{int}}\subset\mathcal{C}^{d}(\mu_{\infty,\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}^{\mathrm{int}})/\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))

is open, which proves the lemma.
∎

Theorem 7.4

(Local Kempf–Ness theorem)
.

The maps
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{\infty}
factorise as continuous maps

\overline{\Gamma}:L_{\mathrm{ss}}/G\longrightarrow B_{2}/K,\qquad\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty}:L_{\mathrm{ss}}/G\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}_{d}/\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h)).

Moreover, for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}
, the restrictions

\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}:B_{1}/\!/\!_{\zeta}G\longrightarrow(\mu_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2})/K,\qquad\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}:B_{1}/\!/\!_{\zeta}G\longrightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{d}(\mu_{\infty,\zeta}^{-1}\{0\})\cap\mathcal{B}_{d})/\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h)),

\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}:B_{1}^{\mathrm{int}}/\!/\!_{\zeta}G\longrightarrow(\mu_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}})/K,\qquad\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}:B_{1}^{\mathrm{int}}/\!/\!_{\zeta}G\longrightarrow(\mathcal{C}^{d}(\mu_{\infty,\zeta}^{-1}\{0\})\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}^{\mathrm{int}})/\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))

are homeomorphisms into their images. Moreover, they have an open image except maybe
\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}
.

Proof.

The existence and the continuity of
\overline{\Gamma}
and
\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty}
is simply the fact that
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{\infty}
are
G
-invariant and continuous by Theorem
7.2
, and the universal property of the quotient topology. Let
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}_{d}
.
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}
,
\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}
,
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}
,
\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}
are continuous as restrictions of continuous functions.

When
(\zeta,b)\in L_{\mathrm{ps}}
, the flow
\phi_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
(resp.
\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,\cdot)
) converges in
G\cdot b\cap B_{2}
toward a zero of
\mu_{\zeta}
(resp.
\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}
), and being integrable is a closed condition so these four functions indeed take their respective values in
(\mu_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2})/K
,
(\mathcal{C}^{d}(\mu_{\infty,\zeta}^{-1}\{0\})\cap\mathcal{B}_{d})/\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))
,
(\mu_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}})/K
and
(\mathcal{C}^{d}(\mu_{\infty,\zeta}^{-1}\{0\})\cap\mathcal{B}_{d}^{\mathrm{int}})/\mathcal{C}^{d+1}(\mathcal{G}(E,h))
.

Similarly, if
\tilde{\Gamma}
is the function introduced in the proof of Theorem
7.2
, we can define
\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}
and
\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\zeta}
. Notice that
\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}=\varphi_{\zeta}\circ\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}
where
\varphi_{\zeta}
is introduced in Lemma
7.3
.

Let,
I_{\zeta}
be the image of
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}
and
\tilde{I}_{\zeta}
the image of
\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\zeta}
. Let

\iota_{\zeta}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle I_{\zeta}&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle B_{1}/\!/\!_{\zeta}G\\
\displaystyle K\cdot b&\longmapsto&\displaystyle G\cdot b\cap B_{1}\end{array}\right.,\qquad\tilde{\iota}_{\zeta}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{\zeta}&\longrightarrow&\displaystyle B_{1}/\!/\!_{\zeta}G\\
\displaystyle K\cdot b&\longmapsto&\displaystyle G\cdot b\cap B_{1}\end{array}\right..

These maps are well-defined because for all
K\cdot b_{\infty}
in the image of
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}
or
\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\zeta}
,
b_{\infty}
is built as a point in the
G
-orbit of some
b\in B_{1}
hence
G\cdot b_{\infty}\cap B_{1}\neq\emptyset
. Moreover, they are continuous.

If
(\zeta,b)\in L_{\mathrm{ps}}
,
\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta}(b,t)
and
\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\phi}_{\zeta}(b,t)
both lie in
G\cdot b
. Therefore,
\iota_{\zeta}
is the left inverse of
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}
and
\tilde{\iota}_{\zeta}
is the left inverse of
\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\zeta}
. It shows that
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}
and
\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\zeta}
are homeomorphisms into their respective images. Moreover, we have
\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}=\varphi_{\zeta}\circ\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}
, thus by Lemma
7.3
,
\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}
is a homeomorphism into its image.

By restriction,
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}
and
\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}
are homeomorphisms into their images. All we have left to show is that the images of
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}
,
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}
and
\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}
are open. Let
K\cdot b_{\infty}=\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}(G\cdot b\cap B_{1})
for some
b\in B_{1}
. By
\mu_{\zeta}
-polystability of
b
, there is a
g\in G
such that
b_{\infty}=g\cdot b
. Let
b_{m}\rightarrow b_{\infty}
be a sequence of zeroes of
\mu_{\zeta}
in
B_{2}
. We have
g^{-1}\cdot b_{m}\rightarrow b\in B_{1}
so for all
m
large enough,
g^{-1}\cdot b_{m}\in B_{1}
. By Corollary
6.5
, for all such
m
,

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta}(g^{-1}\cdot b_{m},t)\in K\cdot\left(\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\phi_{\zeta}(b_{m},t)\right)=K\cdot b_{m}

because
b_{m}
is a zero of
\mu_{\zeta}
. It implies that
b_{m}=\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}(g^{-1}\cdot b_{m})
lies in the image of
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}
if
m
is large enough. Then, notice that the image of
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}
is the image of
\overline{\Gamma}_{\zeta}
intersected with
B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}}
, which is open in
(\mu_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}})/K
.

With the same proof, we show that the image of
\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}
is open in
(\tilde{\mu}_{\zeta}^{-1}\{0\}\cap B_{2}^{\mathrm{int}})/K
. By the second part of Lemma
7.3
, the image of

\overline{\Gamma}_{\infty,\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}=\varphi_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}\circ\overline{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\zeta}^{\mathrm{int}}

is open.
∎

8
Application with the
J
-equation and the dHYM equation on a surface

In this section, we build explicit examples of simple rank
2
bundles on a surface that satisfy the
J
-equation and/or the dHYM equation with respect to a varying metric by using the theorems of the above sections. We start with a few general results on sub-solutions on line bundles when the equation has degree at most
2
. In particular, we show their uniqueness modulo the unitary gauge group.

8.1
General results on surfaces

Proposition 8.1
.

Assume the
P
-critical equation has degree at most
2
i.e.
\zeta_{k}=0
when
k\geqslant 3
. If
(L_{i},h_{i})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant m}
are Hermitian line bundles and for all
i
,
\overline{\partial}_{i}
is a sub-solution to the
P
-critical equation then,
\overline{\partial}_{E}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}\overline{\partial}_{i}
on
E=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}(L_{i},h_{i})
is a sub-solution too.

Proof.

Let for all
i
,
\hat{F}_{i}=\hat{F}(L_{i},h_{i},\overline{\partial}_{i})
so

\hat{F}(E,h_{E},\overline{\partial}_{E})=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{F}_{1}&0&\ldots&0\\
0&\hat{F}_{2}&\ldots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\ldots&\hat{F}_{m}\end{pmatrix}

Let
x\in X
and
v\in T_{x}^{1,0}X\otimes\mathrm{End}(E_{x})\backslash\{0\}
that we decomposes as

v=\begin{pmatrix}v_{11}&v_{12}&\ldots&v_{1m}\\
v_{21}&v_{22}&\ldots&v_{2m}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
v_{m1}&v_{m2}&\ldots&v_{mm}\end{pmatrix},

with
v_{ij}\in T_{x}^{1,0}X\otimes\mathrm{Hom}(L_{j,x},L_{i,x})
. We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(\hat{F}(E,h_{E},\overline{\partial}_{E})\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v\wedge v^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\hat{F}_{i}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger},

(29)

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v\wedge\hat{F}(E,h_{E},\overline{\partial}_{E})\wedge v^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\hat{F}_{j}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}.

(30)

On the other hand, for all
i
, since we are in rank
1
,

\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}_{i}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})=\hat{F}_{i}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}.
