Then, by using the commutativity inside the trace, the symmetric product
\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}(E,h_{E},\overline{\partial}_{E}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v,v^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})
is an average whose half of the terms equals (
29
) and the other half of the terms equals (
30
). Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}(E,h_{E},\overline{\partial}_{E}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v,v^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\hat{F}_{i}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\hat{F}_{j}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}_{i}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})+\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}_{j}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}}).

We deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}([\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}^{\prime}(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},h_{E})\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v\wedge v^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle=2\zeta_{2}\wedge\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}(E,h_{E},\overline{\partial}_{E}),\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v,v^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})+\zeta_{1}\wedge\mathrm{tr}([\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v,v^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle=\zeta_{2}\wedge\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}_{i}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})+\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}_{j}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})\right)

\displaystyle+\zeta_{1}\wedge\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}2\zeta_{2}\wedge\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}_{i}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})+\zeta_{1}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}2\zeta_{2}\wedge\mathrm{tr}([\hat{F}_{j}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}]_{\mathrm{sym}})+\zeta_{1}\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}[\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}^{\prime}(L_{i},\overline{\partial}_{i},h_{i})\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}]+[\mathcal{P}_{\zeta}^{\prime}(L_{j},\overline{\partial}_{j},h_{j})\wedge\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}v_{ij}\wedge v_{ij}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle>0.

Thus,
\overline{\partial}_{E}
is a sub-solution on
(E,h_{E})
.
∎

Corollary 8.2
.

If a solution to the
P
-critical equation exists on a Hermitian line bundle
(L,h)
and the equation has degree at most
2
(in particular, when
X
is a surface), the solution is unique modulo
\mathcal{G}(L,h)
.

Proof.

It is a consequence of Propositions
8.1
and
2.9
.
∎

8.2
An example

Let
X=\mathrm{Bl}_{p}\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
\pi:X\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{2}
the projection,
H_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}\in H^{1,1}(\mathbb{P}^{2})
the hyperplane class,
H=\pi^{*}H_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}\in H^{1,1}(X)
its pull-back and
D\in H^{1,1}(X)
the class of the exceptional divisor
\mathrm{Ex}
. Recall that
H^{2}=1
,
D^{2}=-1
and
H\cup D=0
. In particular, for all
a>b>0
integers,

(aH-bD)^{2}=a^{2}-b^{2}>0,\qquad(aH-bD)\cup(H-E)=a-b>0,\qquad(aH-bD)\cup D=b>0.

(31)

The cone of curves of
X
is generated by
H-D
and
D
. Therefore, the three inequalities (
31
) show that
\mathcal{O}_{X}(bH-aD)
satisfies the Nakai–Moishezon criterion.

In particular, there is a Kähler form
\omega\in 2H-D
and the line bundles
\mathcal{L}_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(13H-11D)
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(6H-2D)
are ample. We have

\frac{[\omega]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{1})}{2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(\mathcal{L}_{1})}=\frac{(2H-D)\cup(13H-11D)}{(13H-11D)^{2}}=\frac{5}{16},

\frac{[\omega]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{2})}{2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(\mathcal{L}_{2})}=\frac{(2H-D)\cup(6H-2D)}{(6H-2D)^{2}}=\frac{5}{16}.

In other words, the constant for the
J
-equation with respect to
\omega
of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
is the same. Let
c_{J}=\frac{5}{16}
. The
J
-equation on these bundles is given by

c_{J}\hat{F}^{2}-\omega\wedge\hat{F}=0.

Let us show that both of these bundles admit a solution to this equation. We use Song’s criterion
[
43
, Corollary 1.2]
which says that
\mathcal{L}_{i}
(
i\in\{1,2\}
) admits a solution to the
J
-equation if and only if for all curve
C\subset X
,

\frac{[\omega]\cup[C]}{\mathrm{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i})\cup[C]}<2c_{J}=\frac{5}{8}.

With the same argument as previously, it is enough to verify it when
[C]=H-D
and
[C]=D
. We have

\frac{[\omega]\cup(H-D)}{\mathrm{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{1})\cup(H-D)}=\frac{(2H-D)\cup(H-D)}{(13H-11D)\cup(H-D)}=\frac{1}{2}<\frac{5}{8},

\frac{[\omega]\cup D}{\mathrm{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{1})\cup D}=\frac{(2H-D)\cup D}{(13H-11D)\cup D}=\frac{1}{11}<\frac{5}{8},

\frac{[\omega]\cup(H-D)}{\mathrm{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{2})\cup(H-D)}=\frac{(2H-D)\cup(H-D)}{(6H-2D)\cup(H-D)}=\frac{1}{4}<\frac{5}{8},

\frac{[\omega]\cup D}{\mathrm{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{2})\cup D}=\frac{(2H-D)\cup D}{(6H-2D)\cup D}=\frac{1}{2}<\frac{5}{8}.

We deduce that both
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
admit a solution to the
J
-equation. Moreover, these respective solutions are automatically
J
-positive (
i.e.
are sub-solutions) by
[
44
, Proof of Theorem 1.1]
. Let
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
be metrics on these line bundles such that the curvatures
\hat{F}_{1}
of
\mathcal{L}_{1}=(L_{1},h_{1},\overline{\partial}_{1})
and
\hat{F}_{2}
of
\mathcal{L}_{2}=(L_{2},h_{2},\overline{\partial}_{2})
satisfy, for
i\in\{1,2\}
,

c_{J}\hat{F}_{i}^{2}-\omega\wedge\hat{F}_{i}=0.

Therefore,
\mathcal{E}_{0}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{2}
satisfies the same equation and by Proposition
8.1
, it is
J
-positive.

Now, let
\alpha
be a closed
(1,1)
-form in
H-2D
. Let
\varepsilon_{1}>0
and
\varepsilon_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. Let

\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}=\omega+\varepsilon_{2}\alpha\in 2H-D+\varepsilon_{2}(H-2D).

If
\left|\varepsilon_{2}\right|
is small enough, it is a positive form. Let us consider the dHYM equation with respect to the Kähler form
\varepsilon_{1}\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}
on some holomorphic Hermitian bundle
\mathcal{E}=(E,h,\overline{\partial})
of curvature
\hat{F}
. Assume moreover that
\mathcal{E}
satisfies Takahashi’s condition for the
J
-equation with respect to
[\omega]

i.e.

\mathrm{ch}_{2}(E)>0
and
[\omega]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(E)>0
.

\displaystyle\Im(\overline{Z_{\mathrm{dHYM},\varepsilon_{1}\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}}(E)}(\varepsilon_{1}\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathrm{Id}_{E}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\hat{F})^{2})

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\Im\left(\overline{(\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathrm{e}}^{-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}[\varepsilon_{1}\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]}\cup\mathrm{ch}(E)})_{(2,2)}(\varepsilon_{1}\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathrm{Id}_{E}+\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\hat{F})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\Im\left(\left(-\mathrm{ch}_{2}(E)-\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\varepsilon_{1}[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(E)+\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\frac{[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]^{2}}{2}\mathrm{rk}(E)\right)(-\hat{F}^{2}+2\boldsymbol{\mathrm{i}}\varepsilon_{1}\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}\wedge\hat{F}+\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{Id}_{E})\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}([\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(E))\hat{F}^{2}-2\varepsilon_{1}\mathrm{ch}_{2}(E)\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}\wedge\hat{F}+\varepsilon_{1}^{3}([\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]^{2}\mathrm{rk}(E))\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}\wedge\hat{F}-\varepsilon_{1}^{3}([\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(E))\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{Id}_{E}.

Let us set

c_{J}(E,\varepsilon_{2})=\frac{[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(E)}{2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(E)},\qquad c_{\mathrm{HYM}}(E,\varepsilon_{2})=\frac{[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(E)}{[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]^{2}\mathrm{rk}(E)},\qquad C(E,\varepsilon_{2})=\frac{[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]^{2}\mathrm{rk}(E)}{2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(E)}.

After a rescaling by
\frac{1}{2\varepsilon_{1}\mathrm{ch}_{2}(E)}
, the dHYM equation for
\mathcal{E}
with respect to the metric
\varepsilon_{1}\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}
is given by

c_{J}(E,\varepsilon_{2})\hat{F}^{2}-\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}\wedge\hat{F}+C(E,\varepsilon_{2})\varepsilon_{1}^{2}(\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}\wedge\hat{F}-c_{\mathrm{HYM}}(E,\varepsilon_{2})\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{Id}_{E})=0.

(32)

The positivity conditions on the Chern classes of
E
and on
\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}
imply that all the terms in (
32
) are well-defined.

We find here a usual result : the
J
-equation is the low-volume limit of the dHYM equation
i.e.
the
J
-equation with respect to
\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}
is the limit equation of the dHYM equation with respect to
\varepsilon_{1}\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}
when
\varepsilon_{1}\rightarrow 0
. In the large volume limit, we find the HYM equation with respect to
\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}
,

\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}\wedge\hat{F}-c_{\mathrm{HYM}}(E,\varepsilon_{2})\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{Id}_{E}=0.

As the sum of two ample bundles,
\mathcal{E}_{0}
satisfies
\mathrm{ch}_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{0})>0
and
\mathrm{ch}_{1}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\cup[\omega]>0
. More generally, any other bundle with the same topology satisfies these conditions. Let us build simple deformations of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
and let us determine for which values of
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
they admit a solution to (
32
).

Lemma 8.3
.

For all integers
(a,b)
with
b\geqslant\max\{a+2,1\}
,
H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(aH-bD))\neq 0
.

Then, the global sections of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(aH-(b-1)D)
are the global sections of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(a)
which vanish at
p\in\mathbb{P}^{2}
with multiplicity at least
b-1
. Since
b-1>a
, there are no such sections except to zero section. Similarly with
\mathcal{O}_{X}(aH-bD)
. Then, by exactness of
\iota_{*}
and because
b>0
,

H^{0}(X,\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Ex}}(b-1))=H^{0}(\mathrm{Ex},\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Ex}}(b-1))\cong\mathbb{C}^{b}\neq 0,

H^{1}(X,\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Ex}}(b-1))=H^{1}(\mathrm{Ex},\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Ex}}(b-1))=0.

We deduce that we have an exact sequence

.

and

Proof.

Let us verify that

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{1})=H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\lor})=H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(7H-9D))

and

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})=H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\lor})=H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(-7H+9D))

are non-zero. For the first one, we simply apply Lemma
8.3
. For the second one, by
[
26
, Proposition 2.4.3]
and
[
26
, Proposition 2.5.5]
, the canonical bundle of
X
is given by
K_{X}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(-3H+D)
and by Serre duality,

H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(-7H+9D))=H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(-7H+9D)^{\lor}\otimes K_{X})=H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(4H-8D)).

We conclude with Lemma
8.3
. It means that we can choose
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
as wanted such that the associated exact sequences don’t split. Since both
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
are simple, a sufficient condition for
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
to be simple is
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})=\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{1})=0
, which is the case by Proposition
2.10
(or simply by noticing that neither
\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\lor}
nor its dual is nef).
∎

Let
\overline{\partial}_{1}
and
\overline{\partial}_{2}
be any complex structures on
E
such that for
i\in\{1,2\}
,
(E,\overline{\partial}_{i})=\mathcal{E}_{i}
. Since the
\mathcal{E}_{i}
are built as extensions of
\mathcal{L}_{2}
by
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
by
\mathcal{L}_{2}
, the
\overline{\partial}_{i}
can be chosen arbitrarily close to
\overline{\partial}_{0}
for the
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
topology.

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
A(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})=\varepsilon_{2}+\frac{1}{128}(1-\varepsilon_{2}^{2})(5-11\varepsilon_{2})\varepsilon_{1}^{2}
. There are
\varepsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for all
0\leqslant\varepsilon_{1}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}
and
-\varepsilon_{0}\leqslant\varepsilon_{2}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}
,

1.

\mathcal{E}_{0}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{2}
admits a solution
\overline{\partial}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)\cdot\overline{\partial}_{0}
to (
32
) with
\left\|\overline{\partial}-\overline{\partial}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}\leqslant\epsilon_{0}
if and only if
A(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})=0
,

2.

\mathcal{E}_{1}
admits a solution
\overline{\partial}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)\cdot\overline{\partial}_{1}
to (
32
) with
\left\|\overline{\partial}-\overline{\partial}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}\leqslant\epsilon_{0}
if and only if
A(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})<0
,

3.

\mathcal{E}_{2}
admits a solution
\overline{\partial}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)\cdot\overline{\partial}_{2}
to (
32
) with
\left\|\overline{\partial}-\overline{\partial}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}\leqslant\epsilon_{0}
if and only if
A(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})>0
.

Proof.

Let
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
be the respective smooth structures of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
. Let
E=L_{1}\oplus L_{2}
be the common smooth structure of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
. We have, for all
\varepsilon_{2}
,

\displaystyle[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(L_{1})

\displaystyle=(2H-D+\varepsilon_{2}(H-2D))\cup(13H-11D)=15-9\varepsilon_{2},

\displaystyle[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(L_{2})

\displaystyle=(2H-D+\varepsilon_{2}(H-2D))\cup(6H-2D)=10+2\varepsilon_{2},

\displaystyle[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(E)

\displaystyle=[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(L_{1})+[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(L_{2})=25-7\varepsilon_{2},

\displaystyle[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]^{2}

\displaystyle=(2H-D+\varepsilon_{2}(H-2D))^{2}=3-3\varepsilon_{2}^{2}.

And

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(L_{1})

\displaystyle=(13H-11D)^{2}=48,

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(L_{2})

\displaystyle=(6H-2D)^{2}=32,

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(E)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(L_{1})+2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(L_{2})=80.

Let
\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}\in H^{*,*}(X,\mathrm{End}(E))
such that the
P_{\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}}
-critical equation for
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
is (
32
). We have
P_{\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}}(E)=0
and

\displaystyle P_{\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}}(L_{1})

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle c_{J}(E,\varepsilon_{2})2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(L_{1})-[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(L_{1})+C(E,\varepsilon_{2})\varepsilon_{1}^{2}([\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(L_{1})-c_{\mathrm{HYM}}(E,\varepsilon_{2})[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]^{2}\mathrm{rk}(L_{1}))

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(E)}{2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(E)}2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(L_{1})-[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(L_{1})+\frac{[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]^{2}\mathrm{rk}(E)}{2\mathrm{ch}_{2}(E)}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\left([\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(L_{1})-[\omega_{\varepsilon_{2}}]\cup\mathrm{ch}_{1}(E)\frac{\mathrm{rk}(L_{1})}{\mathrm{rk}(E)}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{25-7\varepsilon_{2}}{80}48-(15-9\varepsilon_{2})+\frac{(3-3\varepsilon_{2}^{2})2}{80}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\left(15-9\varepsilon_{2}-(25-7\varepsilon_{2})\frac{1}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{24}{5}\varepsilon_{2}+\frac{3}{80}(1-\varepsilon_{2}^{2})(5-11\varepsilon_{2})\varepsilon_{1}^{2}.

We deduce that

P_{\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}}(L_{1})<(\leqslant)\ 0\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad A(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})=\varepsilon_{2}+\frac{1}{128}(1-\varepsilon_{2}^{2})(5-11\varepsilon_{2})\varepsilon_{1}^{2}<(\leqslant)\ 0.

(34)

Let
V=H^{0,1}(X,\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{E}_{0}))
and
B_{2}\subset V
a small enough ball centred at
0
so we can apply Theorem
4.12
at
\mathcal{E}_{0}
. Let
\mathcal{B}_{1}
be the neighbourhood of
\overline{\partial}_{0}
for the
\mathcal{C}^{1}
topology,
\mathcal{U}_{1}
the neighbourhood of
\zeta_{0,0}
for the
\mathcal{C}^{0}
topology and
B_{1}\subset B_{2}
an other ball centred at
0
given by this theorem.

We may assume that
\mathcal{B}_{1}
is the ball of centre
\overline{\partial}_{0}
and radius
\epsilon_{0}>0
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that, when
0\leqslant\varepsilon_{1}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}
and
-\varepsilon_{0}\leqslant\varepsilon_{2}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}
,
\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}\in\mathcal{U}_{1}
. Since
\overline{\partial}_{1}
and
\overline{\partial}_{2}
can be chosen arbitrarily close to
\overline{\partial}_{0}
and are integrable, by Proposition
4.3
, there are
b_{1}\in B_{1}
and
b_{2}\in B_{1}
such that for each
i
,
\overline{\partial}_{i}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathbb{C}}(E)\cdot\overline{\partial}_{b_{i}}
.

The sub-bundles
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
such that
P_{\zeta_{0,0}}(\mathcal{F})=0
are exactly
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
. Therefore, the admissible sub-bundles of
\mathcal{E}_{b_{1}}
are
0
,
L_{1}
and
E
and the admissible sub-bundles of
\mathcal{E}_{b_{2}}
are
0
,
L_{2}
and
E
. We deduce the result of the Proposition by Theorem
4.12
, (
34
) and the fact that for all
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
,
P_{\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}}(L_{2})=P_{\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}}(E)-P_{\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}}(L_{1})=-P_{\zeta_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}}(L_{1})
.
∎
